At contra sancta Ecclesia recta stat, nec unquam incurvari consentit, audiens ab Apostolo: Vigilate, state in fide, viriliter agite, et confortamini, et omnia vestra in charitate fiant (I Cor. XVI, 13). Et Dominus filiis Israel loquitur: Ego sum Dominus Deus vester, qui eripui vos de ergastulo Aegyptiorum, ut incederetis recte (Exod. VI, 8). Statura 1097Aergo Ecclesiae palmae assimilantur, quae in omnibus quae agit, semper in memoria supernam retinet remunerationem. Palma enim victricem ornat manum, et ideo significat praemium illud quod victoribus sanctis in coelestibus est dandum.

Et ubera tua botris. Ubera Ecclesiae (ut superius dictum est) sunt doctores sancti, qui lacte simplicis doctrinae regeneratos in Christo nutriunt. Sed haec ubera botris assimilantur, quando iidem doctores perfectis perfectiora denuntiant, sicut Apostolus loquens simplicioribus dicebat: Non potui vobis loqui tanquam spiritualibus, sed tanquam carnalibus; tanquam parvulis in Christo lac potum vobis dedi, non escam (I Cor. III, 1). Ecce ubera lactis plena; quae ubera in botros sunt versa, cum diceret: Nos praedicamus Christum Dei virtutem et Dei sapientiam, et loquimur sapientiam inter perfectos.

Vers. 8. Dixi: Ascendam in palmam, et apprehendam fructus ejus. Ut ostenderetur omnis haec excellentia et magnitudo donorum Ecclesiae de Dei gratia 1097Bvenire, introducitur idem sponsus dicens: Ascendam in palmam; quasi diceret: Inde habes ubera ad nutriendos parvulos, unde etiam habes botros, quibus perfectioribus perfectiorem propines doctrinam: quia ego mea gratia in te ascendi, et hanc tibi perfectionem concessi. Ascendam (inquit) in palmam. Palma Ecclesiam significat, vel etiam animam cujuscunque fidelis, qui memor est Domini Dei sui, qui dicit: Confidite, ego vici mundum (Joan. XVI, 33). Ipsa quoque omnium vitiorum et iniquitatis victrix esse studet. Bene autem in palmam hic ascendisse dicitur, cum superius in hortum descendisse legatur. Dixit enim Ecclesia: Dilectus meus descendit in hortum suum. Idem est hortus quod et palma, videlicet una eademque Ecclesia. Sed in eam Dominus descendit, et ad eam ascendit: descendit in eam, copiam suae gratiae illi de coelestibus mittendo; ascendit ad illam, dona sua in ea incrementando eamque quibusdam profectibus, veluti quibusdam gradibus, ad 1097Ccelsiora sublevando. Sed et hoc de natura palmae est sciendum, quod in inferioribus aspera est, in superioribus pulcherrima decore foliorum, et pulchritudine fructuum. Sic et sancta Ecclesia in inferioribus asperitatem laborum et passionum sustinet pro Christo: in superioribus vero, hoc est in coelestibus, pulchritudinem et suavitatem exspectat praemiorum. Potest et per palmam arbor dominicae crucis exprimi, in quam Redemptor noster pro humani generis redemptione ascendit, et in qua hostem humani generis superavit. Ait ergo, Ascendam in palmam, id est, per meos prophetas locutus sum, et promisi me ad passionem venturum, et meam Ecclesiam redempturum. Apprehendam (inquit) fructus ejus, id est, fructus bonorum operum et laborum, quibus Ecclesia desudat, remunerando. Vel etiam secundum meum sensum, quo per palmam crux intelligitur: Apprehendam fructus 530 ejus, id est, impleto triumpho passionis, veniam ad gloriam resurrectionis, et apostolis meis spiritualia dona concedam.

1097DEt erunt ubera tua sicut botri vineae. Hoc superius jam expositum est; nam ubera Ecclesiae doctores sunt, cum parvulos simplici doctrina nutriunt: sed botri vineae efficiuntur, cum perfectioribus sublimia quaeque annuntiant. Nam beatus Paulus uno eodemque tempore ubera et botros habuit, quando de Christo loquens dixit: Quorum patres, et ex quibus Christus (Rom. IX, 5); ecce ubera, id est simplex de Christo praedicatio: sed haec ubera in suavissimum et fortissimum vinum conversa sunt, cum subjunxit: Qui est Deus super omnia benedictus in saecula.

Vers. 9. Et odor oris tui sicut malorum. Guttur tuum sicut vinum optimum. Os et odor Ecclesiae est praesens ejus praedicatio, quae fama vel scripto ad absentes ejus pervenit: guttur vero est vox ipsa praedicatoris, quae in praesenti auditur. Odor ergo oris Ecclesiae odoribus malorum comparatur, quia ipsa fama praedicationis plena est suavitatis et gratiae. Guttur vero vino optimo assimilatur, quia ipsa 1098Avox praedicantium magnam in se virtutem auditoribus ostendebat. Quod vero malorum odor in novitate est, vini vero in vetustate, significat praedicationem Ecclesiae et in sui novitate, et etiam in sui perfectione omnem habere suavitatem et gratiam.

Dignum dilecto meo ad potandum, labiisque et dentibus illius ad ruminandum. Vox Ecclesiae est, quae audiens se laudari a sponso, gratulabunda respondet, dicens: Dignum dilecto meo ad potandum; ac si diceret: Sponsus meus guttur meum vino optimo comparavit, et illud vinum dignum est ad potandum ipsi dilecto meo; hoc est praedicatio evangelica, quam in ore meo posuit, per ipsum, et non per alium annuntiari debet in mundo; neque enim per alium decebat mysteria regni coelestis praedicari mundo, quam per Mediatorem Dei et hominum (I Tim. II, 5). Et labiis (inquit) et dentibus illius ad ruminandum. Labia et dentes sponsi, ut supra dictum est, doctores sancti sunt. Illud ergo vinum optimum ruminandum est labiis et dentibus sponsi, quia praedicatio evangelica 1098Btradita a Deo, doctoribus tractanda et exponenda est. Nec absurdum est quod cum vinum potetur et non ruminetur, hic tamen dicitur labiis et dentibus ruminandum, quod magis ad cibum quam ad potum pertinet; nam sancti doctores vinum spirituale ruminant, quando praecepta evangelica diligenter exquirunt, et crebra meditatione investigant atque invicem conferunt.

Vers. 10. Ego dilecto meo, et ad me conversio ejus. Ego dilecto meo, subaudi, obsequium justitiae et devotionis impendo, illi et non alteri fidem et servitutem meam promitto. Et ad me conversio ejus, ut me in praesenti inter labores et aerumnas hujus vitae tueatur, et in futuro ad gaudia vitae coelestis introducat. Potest etiam haec esse vox Synagogae ex persona sanctorum Patrum, adventum Christi desiderantium. Ac si diceret: Scio (inquit) illum spirituali praesentia mihi semper adesse, et ideo ego dilecto meo omni devotione famulari cupio; sed tandem ad me 1098Cconversio ejus, quia opto ut tandem, sicut promisit, in mea substantia mihi appareat. Nam dixerat supra sponsus: Ascendam in palmam, id est promiserat se in carne venturum, et in mea substantia hominem mundo manifestandum.

Vers. 11. Veni, dilecte mi; egrediamur in agrum, commoremur in villis. Ejusdem Synagogae vox est. Veni, dilecte mi, id est appare per assumptum hominem. Tale est et illud Psalmistae: Excita potentiam tuam, et veni (Psal. LXXIX, 3). Et Isaias: Utinam dirumperes coelos, et venires (Isai. LXIV, 1). Egrediamur in agrum. Ager mundus iste est. Egrediamur (inquit) in agrum, id est praedicemus mundo. Commoremur in villis, hoc est ipsis quoque paganis fidem tuam annuntiemus. Nemo nescit Paganos a villa dictos: quia πάγος Graece, villa dicitur Latine; inde Pagani dicti, quia longe sunt a civitate Dei. Potest et Vox Ecclesiae esse: Veni, dilecte mi; ac si diceret: Jam mysterio ascensionis assumptum hominem ad coelos sublevasti; sed veni, divinae majestatis praesentia 1098Dme saepius visitando. Egrediamur in agrum, eodem sensu quo et superius: praedicemus tuae incarnationis fidem mundo. Commoremur in villis, id est etiam ignorantibus te paganis tuam fidem annuntiemus, nec in transitu tantum, sed commoremur ibi, donec illos ex paganis catholicos, ex alienis tibi proprios faciamus.

Vers. 12. Mane surgamus ad vineas. Mane, exortus est novae gratiae, quando videlicet transeuntibus tenebris infidelitatis Evangelium mundo coepit praedicari.

Videamus si floruit vinea, hoc est, inspiciamus Ecclesiam, si jam prima fidei rudimenta suscepit.

Si flores fructus parturiunt. Inspiciamus etiam si flores parturiunt fructum, id est si illi qui jam in Christum crediderunt, idonei sunt ad bene operandum. Nam flores emittit Ecclesia, cum primum fideles ad fidem veniunt: flores vero fructus parturiunt, cum jam ipsa fide et dilectione bene operatur, ne 1099Aipsa fides otiosa sit, vel mortua sine operibus, nam, ut Jacobus dicit: Fides sine operibus mortua est (Jac. II, 26).

Si floruerunt mala punica. Mala punica (ut supra dictum est) rubicundum vel sanguineum habent corticem, et ideo significant passionem Domini, vel membrorum ipsius. Dicit ergo, Si floruerunt mala punica, hoc est si sancti qui jam fide et opere perfecti sunt, idonei etiam sint imitari passionem Christi, et pati pro illo. Mala punica erant illi quibus Apostolus dicebat: Vobis datum est non solum ut in illum credatis, verum etiam ut pro illo patiamini (Philipp. I, 29).

Ibi dabo tibi ubera mea. Ubera Ecclesiae doctores sunt, qui fideles primum ad fidem erudiunt. Cum (inquit) ad vineas exierimus, ibi dabo tibi, o sponse, ubera mea, hoc est, spirituales tibi filios generabo. Unde adhuc subditur.

Vers. 13. Mandragorae dederunt odorem in portis nostris. Mandragora herba est aromatica, cujus radix 1099Bsimilitudinem habet humani corporis. Poma ejus optimi sunt odoris in similitudinem pomi Matiani, quod nostri terrae malum vocant. Haec herba rebus medicinalibus aptissima est. Nam ferunt eos qui incommodo vigiliarum laborant, haustu hujus pomi relevari, et posse dormire. Item ferunt eos qui ob curam secandi sunt, si exteriorem hujus pomi corticem biberint, non sentire sectionem vel adustionem; 531 quae omnia si quis mystice disquirere voluerit, spirituales sensus et Ecclesiae convenientes ibi inveniet. Dicit ergo, Mandragorae dederunt odorem in portis nostris. Portae Ecclesiae sunt apostoli et eorum successores: quia nemo civitatem Dei (id est Ecclesiam) intrat, nisi per sanctos doctores aqua baptismatis regeneratus et vitali doctrina instructus fuerit. De his portis Psalmista dicebat: Diligit Dominus portas Sion super omnia tabernacula Jacob (Psal. LXXXVI, 2). Mandragorae autem fragrantiam virtutum designant. In portis ergo Ecclesiae mandragorae 1099Codorem dederunt, quando apostoli vel successores eorum famam suavissimae opinionis, et odorem virtutum longe lateque sparserunt. Ideoque invitat sponsum suum Ecclesia ut veniat, quia jam nomen ejus longe lateque per apostolos annuntiatur, et odor praedicationis Christi longe lateque dispergitur.

Omnia poma nova et vetera, dilecte mi, servavi tibi, id est praecepta vel promissa Veteris Testamenti et Novi servavi tibi, o dilecte mi. Sciebat enim omnia quae in Veteri Testamento annuntiata sunt, nonnisi per Christum et in Christo posse compleri, ideoque dicebat: Servavi tibi poma nova et vetera, hoc est te exspecto, ut per te impleantur in Novo Testamento quae praedicta sunt in Veteri. Unde et in passione sua jam pendens in cruce, Cum gustasset acetum, dixit, Consummatum est (Joan. XIX, 30); Et post resurrectionem apostolis: Necesse est impleri omnia quae scripta sunt in Lege, et Prophetis, et Psalmis de me (Luc. XXIV, 44). Possunt etiam poma nova et vetera justorum personae intelligi, qui vel in Veteri vel in 1099DNovo Testamento fuerunt. Dicit ergo Ecclesia, Servavi tibi nova et vetera, id est, justos qui in Veteri Testamento praecesserunt, vel in Novo subsecuti sunt, tibi servavi; ut per te laborum suorum praemia perciperent. Nullus enim justorum regnum coelorum intrare valuit, nisi per Christum: quia ipse primus ascendit, et sequentibus suis membris coelum aperuit.

CHAPTER VII.

1093B

Verse 1. What wilt thou see in the Sunamite, but the choirs of the camps? It is the voice of the bridegroom, who, hearing the Synagogue invited to the faith by his bride the Church, says: What wilt thou see in the Sunamite, but the choirs of the camps? as if he were to say: Thou indeed, O bride, grievest over the ruin of the Synagogue, and that she comes late to the faith; but the time is at hand when she shall be converted, and then thou shalt see in her nothing but the choirs of the camps, that is, nothing but the concord of charity and the steadfast strength of faith, wherewith she shall fight for the defense of my faith. For choirs belong to singers: but camps to fighting soldiers. In the Sunamite therefore shall the choirs of the camps be seen, because the Synagogue, when converted, shall both preach Christ in concord with the Church, and shall fight for the faith of Christ even unto death.

How beautiful are thy steps! From this place the bridegroom begins to set forth the praises of his bride. And it is to be noted that he begins to weave her beauty from the steps, and ends her praises at the mouth, as above 1093Che had begun from the eyes. And this he did either to vary the beauty of the song, or also because it is thus most apt for the mysteries. How beautiful (he says) are thy steps! He saw the Church not standing idly in one place, but traversing the whole world with swift preaching, and therefore he began to praise her from her steps.: For the steps of the Church are the swift preaching of the apostles, through whom the Gospel resounded into all the earth.

In sandals, that is, in the examples of the fathers who went before. For sandals are made from dead animals. And the sandals of the Church are the examples of the fathers who went before, by which she has her steps fortified; that by imitating him more safely, and without stumbling, she may walk.

Daughter of the prince. Another translation says, daughter of Aminadab. Aminadab (as was said above) is called the willing one of my people, who rightly holds the figure of Christ. For the Church is both daughter and bride: daughter, because redeemed by his blood, regenerated by his baptism; 1093Dbride, because she is joined to him by the dowry of the Holy Spirit.

The joints of thy thighs are as necklaces that are wrought by the hand of a craftsman. In the thighs, or in the loins, Holy Scripture is wont to designate the propagation of generation. Whence we read of Jacob: All the souls that came out of the thigh of Jacob (Exod. I, 5). The thighs therefore of the Church are the spiritual generation of the believing people through the word of preaching and the washing of regeneration. The joint of the thighs is the joining of the two peoples in one faith, the Jewish and the Gentile. And well is this joint compared to necklaces: because the faith of both peoples is chiefly made manifest in works; for faith without works is dead (Jac. II, 26). And they are wrought by the hand of a craftsman, that is, of almighty God, by whose gift the power of good work is granted to believers. This is that craftsman of whom Paul spoke, when he made mention of the patriarch Abraham: 1094AFor he looked for an abiding city, whose builder and craftsman is God (Hebr. XI, 10).

Verse 2. Thy navel is a turned bowl, never wanting cups. The navel is the frailest part of the body, and therefore by the navel we ought to understand the frailty of our mortality. Now a bowl is a larger cup having handles on this side and on that. The navel therefore of the Church is a turned bowl: because the more the teachers of the Church remember their own frailty, the more do they press on with the word of preaching; that, offering the alms of the word of God, they may themselves obtain mercy from God. And well is it called a turned bowl: for with the lathe we work more easily; by which is signified the readiness of paying out the word, or of giving alms. For alms are not to be given peevishly, nor with hesitation; according to what Solomon says: Say not to thy friend: Go, and come again, tomorrow I will give to thee, when thou canst give at once (Prov. III, 26). That also which is made on the lathe needs no rule or line applied from without: but is unerringly completed of itself. The navel therefore of the Church is a turned 1094Bbowl, because whoever either preaches or gives alms ought to do this for love alone and for the expectation of the heavenly me cedis. For whoever either preaches or has pity on his neighbors for the sake of temporal recompense or of praise, is a bowl, but not a turned one. And this bowl never wants cups, because to preachers the preaching of the word of God is never lacking: because he himself whom they preach supplies them with the cups of knowledge and the strength of constancy; whence also the Psalmist: The Lord shall give the word to them that preach the gospel with great power (Psal. LXVII, 12).

528 Thy belly is like a heap of wheat, set about with lilies. The same frailty which was signified by the navel is shown in this place by the belly, because the belly is guarded by no firmness of bones. Well therefore is the belly of the Church compared to a heap of wheat, because the more each of the saints is mindful of his own frailty, the more zealously does he press on to bring forth the fruits of good works. And rightly he says, not an abundance of wheat, but a heap 1094Cof wheat, that by this he may signify the increases of the virtues, which like a heap climb by growing from the lower to the higher parts. For as a heap spreads out below in broad amplitude, but above is drawn together and pointed in a certain narrowness: so in the Church there are many who live more indulgently, but few who press on to the highest perfection. For more are found who give alms out of their goods than who leave their goods for God. This heap is said to be set about with lilies, because the saints do the good things which they do out of desire of the brightness and whiteness above. For by the lilies that heavenly brightness is signified. By the belly of the Church can also be taken the divine font of baptism, in which the faithful are regenerated into a new creature. This belly therefore is compared to a heap of wheat set about with lilies, because those regenerated at the font of baptism are taught to sweat over good works, and always to gape after the beholding of the glory of the brightness above.

Verse 3. Thy two breasts are like two twin fawns 1094Dof a roe. The breasts of the Church (as has already been said above) signify the holy teachers, who instruct all the little ones (that is, those newly regenerated in Christ) with the milk of a plainer and easier teaching. And well are they called two breasts, because out of two peoples, namely the Jewish and the Gentile, the faithful are gathered, and are nourished with the milk of spiritual teaching. These two breasts are said to be like two twin fawns of a roe. The two fawns are the two Testaments, upon which all the preaching of those same teachers rests, of which Testaments the one author and promulgator is Christ, according to what Solomon says: The words of the wise are as goads, and as bright ones fixed deep on high, which are given by one shepherd (Eccli. XII, 11), namely Christ. For the roe rightly signifies Christ, because both in sight and in swiftness it excels the other living creatures, and it is a clean animal, dividing the hoof and chewing the cud. For such are the members of Christ, namely the teachers, marked out by keenness 1095Aof knowledge, and forsaking earthly things with that same swiftness, and hastening to the things of heaven. Dividing the hoof, that is, having discernment of good and evil; and chewing the cud, that is, meditating on the precepts of God with diligent memory.

Verse 4. Thy neck is as a tower of ivory. The holy teachers, who are signified by the breasts, are themselves signified also by the neck. For both through the arteries of the neck the voice goes forth, and by the service of the neck vital nourishment is supplied to the other members: so also the holy teachers both announce the words of life to the peoples, and nourish them with the food of heavenly teaching. The neck therefore of the Church is compared to a tower of ivory: because the holy teachers furnish that same Church both with beauty and with strength; with beauty indeed, which is signified by the whiteness of the ivory, by living chastely and sincerely; and they furnish strength, which is signified by the tower, when they fortify that same Church against the temptations of the devil or the assaults of heretics.

Thy eyes are as the fishpools in Hesebon, which are in the gate 1095Bof the daughter of the multitude. That is, those same teachers, who were signified by the neck, are signified also by the eyes. For they, as eyes, provide for the whole body of the Church, and show the way which it ought to enter. These eyes of the Church are compared to fishpools built in the gate of the city of Hesebon, because the holy teachers wash the peoples believing in Christ with the life-giving washing, and refresh them with the drink of saving doctrine. And Hesebon is called daughter of the multitude according to the letter, on account of the multitude of peoples flowing together thither. So also the holy Church is rightly called daughter of the multitude, because daily she gathers a great number of the nations. And Hesebon is interpreted a girdle of sorrow. The eyes therefore of the Church are compared to the fishpools of Hesebon, because the holy teachers, having set aside the fleeting joys of the world, bind themselves with the girdle of sorrow: because they live chastely and soberly in this world (Tit. II, 12), and weep for their own wanderings; that mourning in this present world they may be able to come to true and eternal joys. And in the gate 1095Dof Hesebon these fishpools are said to be: because no one is able to enter the Church, unless he be first washed with the water of baptism, and unless he be given to drink of the font of saving doctrine. This also signified the brazen laver which Solomon had set in the porch of the temple, that the priests who were to enter the temple and to immolate the victims might there wash their bodies (III Reg. VII, 23).

Thy nose is as the tower of Lebanon, which looks toward Damascus. Because by the nose we distinguish stenches and good odors, rightly by the nose of the Church are understood those same holy teachers. For they have known how sagaciously to distinguish the fragrant teaching of the catholic faith and the death-bearing stench of heretical error. For among the divine gifts also, which are distributed through the Holy Spirit, it is said: To another is given by the Spirit the discerning of spirits (I Cor. XII, 10). The nose therefore of the Church is compared to the tower of Lebanon, because the holy teachers both hold the highest place in the Church, and stand as it were upon the mountain of Lebanon, and defend the Church from the assaults of the malignant spirits. This tower is said to be toward 1095DDamascus. Damascus is the metropolitan city of Syria, which of old fought against the children of Israel, having as it did most mighty and most cruel kings. Now Damascus is interpreted a drink of blood, or an eye of blood. They report that Abel was slain there, and therefore it signifies the powers of this world, which thirst for a drink of blood: because they are delighted with the pleasures and enticements of flesh and blood. It signifies also the aeras powers, which thirst for the gore of our souls. This tower therefore is against Damascus: because this Church, fortified by the help of Christ, always resists the devil and his members.

Verse 5. Thy head is as Carmel. The head, therefore, of the Church is understood to be the principal part of the mind of the faithful; for as by the head the rest of the members are ruled, so by the mind the thoughts are ordered. And fittingly is the head of the Church compared to Carmel. For Carmelus, which is also called Carmel, is interpreted knowledge or science of circumcision. 1096ATherefore the head of the Church is likened to Carmel, because it knows that circumcision, once celebrated bodily, must now be observed by it spiritually, according to what the prophet says: Be ye circumcised to the Lord in your hearts (Jer. IV, 2). This understanding of circumcision the Jews did not have, and therefore they are rebuked by the blessed Stephen, the protomartyr, 529 who says: Ye stiffnecked and uncircumcised in hearts and ears, ye have always resisted the Holy Spirit (Act. VII, 51). In the book of Kings too it is read (III Reg. XVIII, 45) that Elias went up into mount Carmel, and there by his prayer watered the dry earth with heavenly rains after a long drought of three years and a half. Which history breathes a beautiful allegory; for Elias, who is interpreted my God the Lord, signifies Christ, who, though he is God and Lord of all and of the whole creation, is in a special way her own, that is, is the proper God and Lord of the Church. He goes up into Carmel when by his grace he climbs the minds of his faithful, and through them bestows upon the parched world the rains of his graces. 1096BRightly therefore (as has been said) is the head of the Church compared to mount Carmel: because the mind of the saints is lofty by a certain manner of life, and offers Christ an ascent into itself through the increase of the virtues.

And the hair of thy head as the purple of the king bound in the channels. If by the head of the Church the mind is understood, by the hair of the head we ought to understand the thoughts of the mind, which continually issue from it. But the purple colour, which has the appearance of blood, signifies the mystery of the Lord's passion. According to the letter, what he says, the purple of the king bound in the channels, signifies wool already wrought into the purple colour and not yet drawn out into threads, but still set in the channels, which in Greek they call βάτια, in which the blood of the shellfish is wont to run down and to turn the wool into a purple colour. Mystically these channels signify the humility of the saints, in which the purple of the eternal King is dyed, when all the saints, by imitating the humility of their Redeemer, take pains to be made conformable to his sufferings; so far as they may be turned into 1096Cthe dignity of purple, that is, labouring for Christ, they may deserve to be crowned and to reign with Christ. And this purple is said to be bound, because the hearts of the saints are fixed and established in the fear and love of their Redeemer, nor can they ever be separated from his charity.

Verse 6. How beautiful art thou, and how seemly, most dear one, in delights! How beautiful art thou — namely, in faith — and seemly in work, most dear one, in delights. Above it was said of the same Church: Beautiful as the moon, chosen as the sun, terrible as a battle-line of the camps set in order; but here it is said, beautiful and seemly, most dear one, in delights. Which things seem to be as it were contrary; for to be set in order like a battle-line of the camps, and to be in delights, are at odds, because delights are wont to unnerve the strength of soldiers. But the holy Church does both: she is set in order like a battle-line of the camps, because she resists unbelieving heretics, and terrifies the malignant spirits by her holiness and perfection; she is also in delights, because she continually tastes the sweetness of the heavenly pleasantness with the palate of the mind, and amid 1096Dthe pressures of this world desires to obtain those delights, and to be satisfied with them without ceasing. Whence deservedly she who is such is said to be most dear to her bridegroom Christ.

Verse 7. Thy stature is likened to pulmae. The stature of the Church is her perfect uprightness; for she is not bent down to vices, but stands upright and unmoved. Whence the Psalmist said in the person of sinners: I am bowed down and humbled exceedingly (Psal. CXVIII, 107). And the prophet, under the figure of Jerusalem, says to the sinning soul concerning the malignant spirits: Because they have humbled thee, and have said to thy soul: Bow down, that we may pass over (Isa. LI, 23). But on the contrary the holy Church stands upright, and never consents to be bowed down, hearing from the Apostle: Watch ye, stand fast in the faith, do manfully, and be strengthened, and let all your things be done in charity (I Cor. XVI, 13). And the Lord speaks to the children of Israel: I am the Lord your God, who rescued you out of the workhouse of the Egyptians, that you might walk upright (Exod. VI, 8). The stature 1097Atherefore of the Church are likened to the palm, which in all that she does ever keeps in memory the reward above. For the palm adorns the victorious hand, and therefore signifies that reward which is to be given in the heavens to the saints who conquer.

And thy breasts to clusters of grapes. The breasts of the Church (as was said above) are the holy teachers, who nourish with the milk of simple doctrine those regenerated in Christ. But these breasts are likened to clusters when the same teachers declare more perfect things to the perfect, as the Apostle said, speaking to the simpler sort: I could not speak to you as to spiritual, but as to carnal; as to little ones in Christ I gave you milk to drink, not meat (I Cor. III, 1). Behold breasts full of milk; which breasts were turned into clusters when he said: We preach Christ the power of God and the wisdom of God, and we speak wisdom among the perfect.

Verse 8. I said: I will go up into the palm tree, and will take hold of the fruits thereof. That it might be shown that all this excellence and greatness of the gifts of the Church comes from the grace of God, 1097Bthe same bridegroom is brought in saying: I will go up into the palm tree; as if he should say: From that source thou hast breasts for the nourishing of little ones, from which also thou hast clusters, whereby thou mayest hold out a more perfect doctrine to the more perfect: because I by my grace have gone up into thee, and have granted thee this perfection. I will go up, he says, into the palm tree. The palm signifies the Church, or also the soul of any faithful man who is mindful of the Lord his God, who says: Have confidence, I have overcome the world (Joan. XVI, 33). She too strives to be the conqueror of all vices and of iniquity. And fittingly is he said here to have gone up into the palm, whereas above he is read to have gone down into the garden. For the Church said: My beloved is gone down into his garden. The garden is the same as the palm, namely one and the same Church. But the Lord goes down into her and goes up to her: he goes down into her by sending her from the heavens the abundance of his grace; he goes up to her by increasing his gifts in her and by lifting her up to loftier things 1097Cby certain advances, as it were by certain steps. But this too is to be known of the nature of the palm, that in its lower parts it is rough, in its upper parts most beautiful with the grace of its leaves and the beauty of its fruits. So also the holy Church in her lower parts endures for Christ the roughness of labours and sufferings; but in her upper parts, that is, in the heavens, she awaits the beauty and sweetness of the rewards. The tree of the Lord's cross too may be expressed by the palm, into which our Redeemer went up for the redemption of the human race, and on which he overcame the enemy of the human race. He says therefore, I will go up into the palm tree, that is: I have spoken through my prophets, and have promised that I would come to the passion and would redeem my Church. I will take hold, he says, of the fruits thereof, that is, of the fruits of the good works and labours over which the Church sweats, by rewarding them. Or also according to my sense, whereby the cross is understood by the palm: I will take hold of the fruits 530 thereof, that is, the triumph of the passion being fulfilled, I will come to the glory of the resurrection, and will grant spiritual gifts to my apostles.

1097DAnd thy breasts shall be as the clusters of the vine. This has already been expounded above; for the breasts of the Church are the teachers, when they nourish little ones with simple doctrine; but they are made clusters of the vine when they announce lofty things to the more perfect. For the blessed Paul at one and the same time had breasts and clusters, when speaking of Christ he said: Whose are the fathers, and of whom is Christ (Rom. IX, 5); behold the breasts, that is, the simple preaching concerning Christ: but these breasts were turned into most sweet and most strong wine when he added: Who is God over all, blessed for ever.

Verse 9. And the odour of thy mouth as of apples. Thy throat as the best wine. The mouth and the odour of the Church is her present preaching, which reaches those who are absent from her by report or by writing; but the throat is the very voice of the preacher, which is heard in the present. The odour therefore of the mouth of the Church is compared to the odours of apples, because the very report of her preaching is full of sweetness and grace. But the throat is likened to the best wine, because the very 1098Avoice of those who preach showed in itself great power to its hearers. But that the odour of apples is in newness, and that of wine in age, signifies that the preaching of the Church has all sweetness and grace both in its newness and also in its perfection.

Worthy for my beloved to drink, and for his lips and teeth to ruminate upon. It is the voice of the Church, who, hearing herself praised by the bridegroom, answers with rejoicing, saying: Worthy for my beloved to drink; as if she should say: My bridegroom has compared my throat to the best wine, and that wine is worthy to be drunk by my very beloved; that is, the gospel preaching, which he has placed in my mouth, ought to be announced in the world through him and not through another; for it was not fitting that the mysteries of the kingdom of heaven be preached to the world through any other than through the Mediator of God and men (I Tim. II, 5). And for his lips, she says, and teeth to ruminate upon. The lips and teeth of the bridegroom, as was said above, are the holy teachers. That best wine, therefore, is to be ruminated by the lips and teeth of the bridegroom, because the gospel preaching, 1098Bdelivered by God, is to be handled and expounded by the teachers. Nor is it absurd that, although wine is drunk and not ruminated, yet here it is said to be ruminated by the lips and teeth, which belongs rather to food than to drink; for the holy teachers ruminate the spiritual wine when they diligently search out the gospel precepts, and investigate them by frequent meditation and confer them one with another.

Verse 10. I to my beloved, and his turning is toward me. I to my beloved — understand: I bestow the obedience of justice and devotion; to him and to no other do I promise my faith and my service. And his turning is toward me, that in the present he may protect me amid the labours and hardships of this life, and in the future may bring me into the joys of the heavenly life. This may also be the voice of the Synagogue in the person of the holy Fathers, who longed for the coming of Christ. As if she should say: I know, she says, that he is always present to me by a spiritual presence, and therefore I to my beloved — I desire to serve him with all devotion; but at last his turning is toward me, 1098Cbecause I wish that at last, as he promised, he may appear to me in my own substance. For the bridegroom had said above: I will go up into the palm tree, that is, he had promised that he would come in the flesh, and would be manifested to the world as man in my substance.

Verse 11. Come, my beloved; let us go forth into the field, let us abide in the villages. It is the voice of the same Synagogue. Come, my beloved, that is, appear through the manhood thou hast assumed. Such also is that word of the Psalmist: Stir up thy might, and come (Psal. LXXIX, 3). And Isaias: O that thou wouldst break asunder the heavens, and come (Isai. LXIV, 1). Let us go forth into the field. The field is this world. Let us go forth, he says, into the field, that is, let us preach to the world. Let us abide in the villages, that is, let us announce thy faith to the pagans themselves also. No one is ignorant that Pagans are named from the villa, the village: for πάγος in Greek is called villa in Latin; thence they are called Pagans, because they are far from the city of God. It can also be the voice of the Church: Come, my beloved; as if she should say: Already by the mystery of the ascension thou hast raised to the heavens the manhood thou didst assume; but come, by visiting me 1098Dmore often with the presence of thy divine majesty. Let us go forth into the field, in the same sense as above: let us preach to the world the faith of thy incarnation. Let us abide in the villages, that is, let us announce thy faith even to the pagans who know thee not; and not in passing only, but let us abide there, until we make them catholics out of pagans, and thine own out of strangers.

Verse 12. Let us get up early to the vineyards. The morning is the rising of the new grace, when, namely, the darkness of unbelief passing away, the Gospel began to be preached to the world.

Let us see if the vineyard has flourished, that is, let us look upon the Church, whether she has already received the first rudiments of faith.

If the flowers bring forth fruits. Let us look also whether the flowers bring forth fruit, that is, whether they who have now believed in Christ are fit for working well. For the Church puts forth flowers when the faithful first come to the faith; but the flowers bring forth fruits when she now works well by that very faith and love, lest 1099Athe faith itself be idle, or dead without works; for, as James says: Faith without works is dead (Jac. II, 26).

If the pomegranates have flourished. Pomegranates (as was said above) have a ruddy or blood-coloured rind, and therefore signify the passion of the Lord, or of his members. He says therefore, If the pomegranates have flourished, that is, whether the saints who are now perfect in faith and in work are also fit to imitate the passion of Christ and to suffer for him. Pomegranates were those to whom the Apostle said: To you it is given, not only that you believe in him, but also that you suffer for him (Philipp. I, 29).

There will I give thee my breasts. The breasts of the Church are the teachers, who first instruct the faithful unto the faith. When, she says, we shall have gone out to the vineyards, there will I give thee, O bridegroom, my breasts, that is, I will beget for thee spiritual sons. Whence there is further subjoined:

Verse 13. The mandrakes have given a smell in our gates. The mandrake is an aromatic herb, whose root 1099Bhas the likeness of a human body. Its apples are of the best odour, after the likeness of the Matian apple, which our own people call the apple of the earth. This herb is most apt for medicinal uses. For they report that those who labour under the trouble of sleeplessness are relieved by a draught of this apple, and are able to sleep. Likewise they report that those who for the sake of a cure are to be cut, if they have drunk the outer rind of this apple, do not feel the cutting or the burning; 531 all which things, if anyone should wish to search out mystically, he will find there spiritual senses, and such as befit the Church. He says therefore, The mandrakes have given a smell in our gates. The gates of the Church are the apostles and their successors: because no one enters the city of God (that is, the Church) unless he has been regenerated by the water of baptism through the holy teachers and instructed in life-giving doctrine. Of these gates the Psalmist said: The Lord loveth the gates of Sion above all the tabernacles of Jacob (Psal. LXXXVI, 2). And the mandrakes designate the fragrance of the virtues. In the gates, therefore, of the Church the mandrakes 1099Chave given their smell, when the apostles or their successors scattered far and wide the report of a most sweet repute and the odour of the virtues. And therefore the Church invites her bridegroom to come, because now his name is announced far and wide through the apostles, and the odour of the preaching of Christ is spread far and wide.

All apples, the new and the old, my beloved, I have kept for thee, that is, the precepts or the promises of the Old Testament and of the New I have kept for thee, O my beloved. For she knew that all the things which were announced in the Old Testament could not be fulfilled save through Christ and in Christ, and therefore she said: I have kept for thee apples new and old, that is: I await thee, that through thee there may be fulfilled in the New Testament the things which were foretold in the Old. Whence also in his passion, now hanging on the cross, When he had tasted the vinegar, he said, It is consummated (Joan. XIX, 30); and after the resurrection, to the apostles: All things must needs be fulfilled which are written in the Law, and the Prophets, and the Psalms concerning me (Luc. XXIV, 44). The new and the old apples may also be understood as the persons of the just, who were either in the Old or in 1099Dthe New Testament. The Church says therefore, I have kept for thee the new and the old, that is: the just who went before in the Old Testament, or followed after in the New, I have kept for thee, that through thee they might receive the rewards of their labours. For none of the just was able to enter the kingdom of heaven save through Christ: because he first ascended, and opened heaven for his members who follow.

CAPUT VIII.

Vers. 1. Quis mihi det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera matris meae? Ex his verbis intelligitur totum hoc carmen divinum esse et spirituale, et nihil in se quod juxta litteram intelligi possit, habere. Quae enim unquam amatrix hoc optavit, ut is quem diligit parvulus efficiatur, et ejus frater; et in infantem versus, sugat ubera matris suae? Est ergo vox illius Ecclesiae, quae adventum Christi praecessit, quam Synagogam, id est congregationem appellamus. Quis (inquit) mihi det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera matris 1100Ameae? Et est sensus: Tu qui modo es in sinu Patris (Joan. I, 18), quis det ut homo efficiaris, et particeps ac consors meae naturae existens, frater meus appelleris? Nam ipse se fratrem apostolorum vocare dignatus est, dicens per Psalmistam: Narrabo nomen tuum fratribus meis (Psal. XXI, 23); et post resurrectionem mulieribus: Ite (inquit) nuntiate fratribus meis (Matth. XXVIII, 10). Sugentem ubera matris meae, inquit, hoc est implentem omnia quae naturae humanae conveniunt, excepto peccato. Nam mater Synagogae in hoc loco ipsa natura humana intelligitur, de qua ipsa Synagoga exorta est. Sugentem (inquit) ubera matris meae, id est sustinentem omnia quae humanae fragilitati competunt.

Ut inveniam te foris, hoc est ut hominem factum in aperto videam. Quasi enim intus erat Deus, cum jam in sinu majestatis paternae lateret; sed foris inventus est, quando homo factus visibilem se hominibus praebuit, et incomprehensibilis voluit comprehendi.

Et deosculer te. Et deosculer te, inquit, id est manifesta 1100Bte aspiciam visione, quem nunc tantum in fidei teneo osculo; quod initio hujus carminis optavit, dicens: Osculetur me osculo oris sui (Cant. I, 1), id est per semetipsum jam veniat, ut mihi appareat. Quasi enim per prophetas osculabatur Ecclesiam dilectus, cum adventum suum per eos repromitteret. Optat ergo ut per seipsum veniens illam osculetur, hoc est, ut in carne sua praesentiam exhibeat.

Et jam me nemo despiciat. Quasi enim despecta erat ante Synagoga quam Christus veniret: quia jam legalibus observationibus dedita, sub typo et figura, et non in veritate adventum Christi praedicabat. At postquam Jesus apparuit, et adventus sui gratia mundum illustravit, Jam nemo despexit eam; quinimo terribilis ut castrorum acies hostibus suis fuit: quia per universum mundum Christum praedicavit, et mundanum imperium sibi subdidit. Nec mirum videri debet, si nunc Ecclesiam Synagogam dicimus; una est enim Ecclesia in praecedentibus et 1100Cin sequentibus partibus.

Vers. 2. Apprehendam te, et ducam te in domum matris meae. O dilecte! per fidem et charitatem tibi adhaerendo, ducam te in domum matris meae. Mater Ecclesiae, hoc in loco coelestis illa Jerusalem intelligitur de qua Paulus dicit: Illa autem quae sursum est Jerusalem, libera est, quae est mater nostra (Gal. IV, 26). Ducam te (inquit) in domum matris meae, id est, prosequar te revertentem in coelum, post actum incarnationis tuae et passionis mysterium. Ducam autem te, inquit, pro eo quod est, deducam et prosequar: quia ascendentem Dominum in coelos apostoli oculis deduxerunt, et quousque eum nubes suscepit, pio aspectu prosecuti sunt, donec angeli assistentes dixerunt: Viri Galilaei, quid statis aspicientes in coelum? Hic Jesus qui assumptus est a vobis in coelum, sic veniet quemadmodum vidistis eum euntem in coelum (Act. I, 11).

Ibi me docebis. Hoc ad superiora refertur, ubi dicitur, inveniam te foris. Cum (inquit) te foris invenero, 1100Dhoc est, cum te in assumpta carne aspexero, ibi docebis me: quia ad omnia quae interrogare voluero, respondebis. Nam saepe etiam volentes interrogare apostolos Dominus praeveniebat, eosque instruebat (Marc. IX). Potest et ad hunc locum jungi, ubi dicit, Ducam te in domum matris meae, ibi me docebis. Cum (inquit) coelo receptus fueris, et me quoque in coelum tecum assumpseris, ibi me docebis, et in omnem veritatem induces, quod impossibile est homini in praesenti vita posse consequi. Hinc est quod Dominus repromittit apostolis dicens: Venit hora cum jam non in proverbiis loquar vobis, sed palam de Patre annuntiabo vobis (Joan. XVI, 25). Ergo ibi me docebis, id est, te et Patrem mihi aperta visione revelabis, hoc est divinitatem tuam mihi facie ad faciem ostendes.

Et dabo tibi poculum ex vino condito. Per vinum fervor charitatis et dilectionis intelligitur. 532 Dabo (inquit) tibi vinum, hoc est ostendam quam ferventissima charitate te diligam. Et hoc vinum non simplex 1101Aerit, sed conditum, bonis videlicet operibus et virtutibus. Nam amor Dei, sicut beatus Gregorius dicit (S. Gregorius, homil. 30 in Evang.), nunquam est otiosus, operatur magna si est; si enim operari renuit, amor non est. Vinum enim conditum est dilectio cum operatione. Unde et Dominus in Evangelio: Si quis diligit me, sermonem meum servabit (Joan. XIV, 23). Probatio ergo dilectionis exhibitio est operis.

Et mustum malorum granatorum meorum. Malum granatum idem est quod et malum punicum. Dicitur autem malum granatum, eo quod multitudine granorum sit plenum; malum punicum, eo quod in Africa plurimum abundat. Punica enim ipsa est Africa. Habet autem corticem sanguinei coloris, et ideo (ut saepe dictum est) figuram tenet dominicae passionis. Mustum enim malorum granatorum est dilectio sanctorum martyrum, qui in tantum Deum dilexerunt, ut pro illo mori non dubitarent. Et bene mustum dicitur, non aliud quodlibet poculum, quia mustum 1101Bferventissimum est, ita ut in nimio fervore omnem sordem ex se excoquat et purget. Dabit ergo Ecclesia Dilecto mustum malorum granatorum suorum, id est, ostendet in passionibus sanctorum martyrum quanta charitate Christus diligatur.

Vers. 3. Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me. Per laevam (ut beatus Gregorius dicit) omnia prospera praesentis vitae accipiuntur (S. Greg. in cap. II Cant. cant.); per dexteram vero futura beata vita in coelis accipitur. Laevam ergo sponsi Ecclesia sub capite habet, et dextera illam amplexatur: quia quaecunque prospera praesentis saeculi blandiuntur, pro nihilo deputat: cuncta quae temporaliter arrident, despicit; et dum ad solam supernae beatitudinis visionem tendit, illam solummodo amplexari desiderat.

Vers. 4. Adjuro vos, filiae Jerusalem, ne suscitetis neque evigilare faciatis dilectam, donec ipsa velit. Habet Ecclesia quosdam in se, qui baptismo Christi sunt 1101Crenati, et nomine christianitatis titulati moribus tamen et conversatione saeculo serviunt, et Ecclesiam suavissima sopitam quiete inquietare, atque ad perferendos tumultus excitare nituntur. Hi ergo propter gratiam mysteriorum Christi, quae perceperunt, filiae Jerusalem appellantur in hoc loco, cum tamen moribus contraria agant. Et adjurat eos sponsus ne suscitent vel evigilare faciant dilectam, hoc est, ne illam in somno dulcissimae quietis positam, hoc est in contemplatione sui Conditoris, sua inquietudine excitent, et ad sollicitudines et curas saeculi evigilare compellant. Donec ipsa velit, hoc est donec peracto officio debitae servitutis, qua in psalmis, hymnis et canticis spiritualibus Deo famulatur, admoneatur iterum cura corporeae fragilitatis ad usum saeculi redire.

Vers. 5. Quae est ista quae ascendit de deserto, deliciis affluens, innixa super dilectum suum? Vox Synagogae admirantis Ecclesiam de gentibus congregatam. Quae est, inquit, ista, id est qualis est, quanti meriti, quae ascendit de deserto? Derelicta enim fuerat a Deo 1101Dper idololatriam et veritatis ignorantiam. Ascendebat ergo virtutum gradibus de deserto, hoc est de errore infidelitatis, in quo ad tempus derelicta fuerat. Deliciis affluens, id est virtutibus abundans. Innixa, inquit, super dilectum suum, id est, fiducialiter agens in Christo, et omnes virtutes suas et cuncta bona ad ejus gratiam referens. Sciebat se nihil suis viribus boni implere posse, ideoque cum Apostolo dicit: Gratia Dei sum id quod sum (I Cor. XV, 10). Et notandum quod dicit, super dilectum suum: nam Synagoga sola putabat se habere cognitionem Dei, caeteris gentibus in ignorantia destitutis. Notus enim, ut ait Psalmista, in Judaea Deus, in Israel magnum nomen ejus (Psal. LXXV, 2). Sed ita miranti respondet Synagogae ipsius sponsus et dilectus quod sequitur.

Sub arbore malo suscitavi te. Arborem malum, crucem dominicam debemus accipere, sub qua arbore suscitata est Synagoga: quia ipsa a praevaricatione originalis peccati, et a potestate diaboli per crucem 1102AChristi redempta est: ibi suscitata est quae peccatis mortua erat. Et bene arborem mali dicit; nam et supra dilectum huic arbori Christum comparavit, dicens: Sicut malum inter ligna silvarum, sic dilectus meus inter filios.

Ibi corrupta est mater tua, ibi violata est genitrix tua. Matrem et genitricem Synagogae, majorem et principaliorem Judaicae plebis partem dicit, quales erant scribae et pharisaei, qui Christum Pilato tradiderunt (Matth. XXVII, 2), qui reliquam plebem tanquam mater et genitrix regere debebant et erudire; et quales erant illi qui suadentibus illis principibus improbe acclamabant: Crucifige, crucifige eum (Joan. XIX, 6). Haec ergo mater Synagogae sub arbore malo corrupta est et violata, quando se suosque posteros crudeli maledictione constrinxit, dicens: Sanguis ejus super nos et super filios nostros (Matth. XXVII, 25). Nam et ipsi sub cruce Christi erant, licet non fide et devotione, tamen crudelis vindictae sibi et suis posteris imprecatione.

1102BVers. 6. Pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum, ut signaculum super brachium tuum. Quia (inquit) sub arbore malo suscitavi te, hoc est quia in passione mea in cruce te redemi, Pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum, hoc est habeto me semper in memoria, et ne obliviscaris mei, quanta pro tua salute pertulerim. Pone me (inquit) ut signaculum super cor tuum per fidem, pone me ut signaculum super brachium tuum per dignam operationem. Nam et nos quando aliquid in memoria habere volumus, signaculum digito vel brachio imponere solemus; ut illo admoniti, illius rei, cujus curam habemus, recordemur. Et nos ut signaculum super cor et brachium Christum ponere debemus, ut ejus memoriam semper habeamus, et ut recte in illum credentes, fidem ipsam bonis operibus exornemus. Quod si fecerimus, ipse quoque nos velut signaculum assumet, et nostri recordabitur, juxta quod Zorobabel duci Judaeae repromittit: In die illa suscipiam te, Zorobabel serve meus, et ponam te 1102Csicut signaculum in conspectu meo (Aggaei II, 24). Nam si nos beneficiorum ejus obliti fuerimus, nec ei dignas gratiarum actiones retulerimus, ipse quoque 533 nostri obliviscetur, sicut de impio rege Jechonia dicitur: Si fuerit Jechonias filius Joachim regis Juda, annulus in manu dextera mea, inde evellam eum (Jer. XXII, 24).

Quia fortis est ut mors dilectio, dura ut infernus aemulatio. Dilectio Christi fortis est ut mors. Sicut enim mors animam a corpore separat, et jam nihil concupiscere, nihil in praesenti vita licet ambire: ita dilectio Christi quem vere pervaserit, totum huic saeculo mortificat, et quasi insensibilem reddit, solumque pro Christo vivens, mundo mortuus est. Idem sensus est cum dicit, dura ut infernus aemulatio. Nam sicut infernus eos quos semel recipit, nunquam restituit, sed semper retinet: ita verus Christi amor quem semel acceperit, nunquam dimittit; unde Apostolus dicit: Quis nos separabit a charitate Christi, tribulatio, an angustia, an persecutio, an fames, an 1102Dnuditas, an periculum, an gladius (Rom. VIII, 35)? Sed et dilectio Christi hoc loco potest accipi, cujus dilectio fortis fuit ut mors: quia in tantum dilexit nos, ut pro nobis immortalis mortem susciperet. Dura fuit aemulatio ejus ut infernus, quia infernus nescit misereri, aut a poenis miserorum mitigari: sic et Christi dilectio non potuit a misericordia cessare, aut ullis improbitatibus persecutorum removeri ab amore humani generis. Quidam hoc quod dicitur, dura est ut infernus aemulatio, ad Synagogam proprie referunt, quae gentes aemulabatur, et invidebat saluti eorum. Monet ergo Christus hoc dicens, ut ab invidia cesset, et saluti Ecclesiae congaudeat.

Lampades ejus, lampades ignis atque flammarum. Id est, dilectionis lampades sunt lampades ignis atque flammarum. Lampades dilectionis sunt praecordia sanctorum, in quibus veluti in vasis dilectio habitat. Hae ergo lampades sunt ignis et flammarum: lampades quidem sunt ignis, quia in corde ardent 1103Aper amorem: lampades vero flammarum, quia exterius lucent per operationem.

Vers. 7. Aquae multae non poterunt exstinguere charitatem, nec flumina obruent illam. Aquae et flumina sunt violentae persecutorum minae, vel etiam blandimenta, quibus sanctos a charitate Dei separare nituntur. De quibus Dominus in Evangelio dicit, de viro qui aedificavit domum suam supra petram: Venerunt flumina, flaverunt venti, et impegerunt in domum illam, et non cecidit; fuit enim aedificata supra petram (Matth. VII, 25). Aquae ergo multae non poterunt exstinguere charitatem, quia nulla tribulatio, nulla angustia sanctos a charitate Dei separare valebit.

Si dederit homo omnem substantiam domus suae pro dilectione, quasi nihil despiciet eam. Hic versiculus non eget expositione; nam nullius mundi substantiae, nullae opes comparatione dilectionis aliquid sunt. Legimus quidem sanctos sua dimisisse pro amore Christi, sed tamen nihil sibi videbantur dimittere in 1103Bcomparatione charitatis Christi, quem amabant. Unde Apostolus cum non solum terrenam substantiam, sed et paternas traditiones dimitteret pro Christo, dicebat: Haec omnia contemno et arbitror ut stercora, ut Christum lucrifaciam (Philip. III, 8), id est, ut amorem illius acquiram.

Vers. 8. Soror nostra parva est, et ubera non habet. Quid faciemus sorori nostrae in die quando alloquenda est? Vox dilecti Synagogam alloquentis pro Ecclesia gentium. Soror nostra, inquit, id est gentium Ecclesia, parva est, et ubera non habet; et magna dignatione communicato cum Synagoga nomine, dicit, Soror nostra; quia enim ex Judaeis carnem assumpsit, ideo se quasi Synagogae contemperat et exaequat. Parva est (inquit) soror nostra. Sicut alicujus hominis aetas describitur, sic et aetas describitur Ecclesiae. Parva enim erat Ecclesia adhuc de gentibus congregata sub ipsis Domini et apostolorum temporibus: quia et parvitate credentium parva existebat; 1103Cet ubera necdum habebat, id est, nequaquam praedicando spirituales Deo filios generare valebat. Dicit ergo quasi consilium Synagogae requirens: Quid faciemus sorori nostrae in die quando alloquenda est? Allocutio Ecclesiae est, vel cum intrinsecus occulta inspiratione, vel exterius aperta praedicatione admonet Deus animam cujusque fidelis. Et est sensus; quando per apostolos meos alloquar Ecclesiam de gentibus congregatam, quid faciemus? quomodo illi praecepta coelestia committemus? Utrum videlicet minima quaeque praecepta illi committemus, an quo major efficiatur, perfectiora illi secretorum coelestium mysteria trademus? Nam Deus omnipotens pro mensura et capacitate mentis humanae temperat verbum praedicationis, considerans vires et capacitatem auditoris. Sed Synagoga tacente, ipse sponsus pro ea respondit.

Vers. 9. Si murus est, aedificemus super eum propugnacula argentea; si ostium est, compingamus illud tabulis cedrinis. Si (inquit) soror nostra est murus, 1103Daedificemus super eum propugnacula argentea. Ac si dicat: Cum coeperint apostoli mei praedicare Ecclesiae de gentibus congregatae, si tales fuerint auditores, qui merito perfectionis murus appellari possint, dum videlicet vel naturali ingenio praediti, vel philosophica eruditione praestantes, quasi alios defendere et munire possint vice muri, aedificemus super hunc talem murum propugnacula argentea, id est, addamus illis eloquia divinarum Scripturarum. Nam, ut Psalmista dicit: Eloquia Domini eloquia casta, argentum igne examinatum (Psal. XI, 7). Si vero tales fuerint auditores qui nequaquam hostibus resistere possint, nec tela contra venientia repellere, sed fuerint velut ostium, ut simplicia annuntiantes, possint aliquos in Ecclesiam et ad fidem adducere, quales sunt sacerdotes minoris gradus, qui baptizando in Ecclesia, et divinis mysteriis initiando homines in Ecclesiam introducunt, compingamus illud ostium tabulis cedrinis, id est, proponamus eis exempla praecedentum 1104APatrum, qui veluti cedri, imputribilia videlicet ligna, id est immarcescibilia opera habent, et veluti tabulae scientiae et charitatis latitudine diffusi sunt. Latitudo enim tabularum latitudinem cordis (in quo debet esse latitudo charitatis vel scientiae) significat, dicente Psalmista: Viam mandatorum tuorum cucurri, cum dilatares cor meum (Psal. CXVIII, 32). His ergo cedrinis tabulis (id est sanctorum exemplis) compingamus illud ostium; ut videlicet illorum imitando exempla, aliis Ecclesiam introeundi veluti ostium, januam aperiant.

Vers. 10. Ego murus, et ubera mea sicut turris. Vox Ecclesiae. Quae cum audisset pro sua salute consulentem dilectum suum, ipsa laeta respondit: Ego sum murus, et ubera mea sicut turris. Ego (inquit) murus: quia ego super firmam petram 534 fundata sum (Matth. XVI, 18), et glutine divinae charitatis solidata. Sive murus sum, quia de vivis et electis lapidibus (hoc est sanctis) aedificata sum. Et ubera mea sicut turris, quia tales intra me contineo, qui 1104Bvice uberum alios nutrire possunt spirituali doctrina, et vice turris possunt alios defendere et munire; quique, sicut turres in muro eminenti, sic ipsi merito doctrinae et conversationis inter reliquos membra mea praecellunt. Hoc autem non meis meritis, neque libertate mei arbitrii habeo, sed dono et gratia sponsi mei.

Ex quo facta sum coram eo quasi pacem reperiens. Id est, ex quo ille sanguinem suum pro me fudit, et repropitiando mihi Deum Patrem, dissolvit inimicitias quae erant inter Deum et hominem, mihique coelestia pacificavit. Ex quo ergo hanc pacem reperi, murus esse et ubera habere vice turrium merui.

Vers. 11. Vinea fuit pacifico, id est dilecto meo, qui est verus pacificus: quia per eum pacificata sunt omnia quae sunt in coelis et in terra. Unde et a propheta: Pater futuri saeculi, Princeps pacis (Isai. IX, 6) appellatur: in cujus veri pacifici typo Salomon, id est pacificus, auctor hujus carminis praecessit. Huic 1104Cergo fuit vinea, id est sancta Ecclesia.

In ea quae habet populos. Hoc dicit, ut se de Ecclesia gentium, non de Synagoga ostenderet canere. Nam Synagoga unius gentis fuit tantum, Ecclesia vero habet populos: quia omnes gentes intra capacissimum suum gremium capit et recipit.

Tradidit eam custodibus. Custodes hujus vineae (id est sanctae Ecclesiae) sunt prophetae, apostoli, apostolorumque successores. Sive etiam custodes hujus saeculi sunt angelicae virtutes, quae ab incursibus tentationum et daemonum Ecclesiam custodiunt.

Vir affert pro fructu ejus mille argenteos. Viros fortes dicit et perfectos, nam vir a virtute dicitur. Fructus Ecclesiae est coelestis vitae remuneratio, pro qua sancta Ecclesia in praesenti vita laborare non cessat. Mille autem argentei quos pro fructu vir affert, significant omnia quae in hoc mundo possideri possunt. Millenarius enim numerus pro summa perfectione et plenitudine ponitur. Vir ergo pro fructu hujus vineae mille argenteos affert: quia sancti omnia 1104Dsua relinquunt, et pauperibus erogant; ut fructum hujus vineae (hoc est supernam mercedem) consequantur. Tale est et illud quod Dominus in Evangelii parabola dicit: Simile est regnum coelorum thesauro abscondito in agro, quem qui invenit homo, abscondit, et prae gaudio illius vadit, et vendit omnia quae habet, et emit agrum illum (Matth. XIII, 44).

Vers. 12. Vinea mea coram me est. Vox sponsi. Tu (inquit) dicis quod vineam meam tradidi custodibus: tamen vinea semper coram me est; quia licet eam custodibus meis commiserim, ego tamen semper curam illius habeo, nec pro illa semper sollicitus esse desisto, sed sum cum illa usque ad consummationem saeculi.

Mille tui pacifici, et ducenti his qui custodiunt fructus ejus. Mille subauditur argentei, quos pro vineae dedisti fructu, pacifici tui sunt; quasi diceret: O anima fidelis, noli dubia esse pro aeterna mercede, pro qua omnia tua dedisti: quia mille argentei pacifici 1105Atui sunt, id est, manet te certa illa remuneratio, et verum inde fructum consecutura es. Et ducenti, subaudi, argentei pacifici sunt, his qui custodiunt fructus ejus. Sicut per millenarium plenitudo perfectionis, sic et per centenarium summa perfectio designatur: quia centenarius de laeva in dexteram transfertur. Ducenti ergo argentei, qui est duplicatus centenarius, significant eos qui doctrina et sancta operatione perfecti sunt, qui juste et pie vivendo semetipsos custodiunt, et etiam praedicando alios convertunt. His ergo talibus ducenti argentei sunt pacifici: quia duplicata in coelis eos manet remuneratio; de qualibus per Danielem dicitur: Et qui ad justitiam erudiunt multos, fulgebunt quasi stellae in perpetuas aeternitates (Dan. XII, 3). Hos enim Apostolus dupliciter jubet honorari, dicens: Presbyteri qui in vobis sunt, duplici honore digni habeantur, maxime qui praesunt vobis et laborant in verbo (I Tim. V, 17).

Vers. 13. Quae habitas in hortis, amici auscultant: fac me audire vocem tuam. Vox dilecti sponsam, id 1105Best Ecclesiam alloquentis: O Ecclesia quae habitas in hortis, id est quae excolendis virtutum germinibus insistis. Hortus etenim sponsi ipsa Ecclesia est, quam (ut superius dictum est) crebro se promittit invisere. Ecclesia ergo in hortis habitat: quia plantaria virtutum assidue nutrit; nec transitorie quasi post modicum recessura, ibi tugurium ponit, sed fixa statione ibi habitat. Fac me (inquit) audire vocem tuam, id est, praedica quibus vales Evangelium, praecepta meae legis, simul et promissum coelestium praemiorum annuntia. Hoc enim mihi jucundum et delectabile est, ut audiam vocem tuam, hoc est, ut audiam te verba salutis aeternae annuntiantem. Sed amici auscultant te, id est, angelici spiritus vocem tuam audire delectant, quos tibi amicos feci effusione sanguinis mei, quosque adjutores et protectores contra malignos spiritus institui. Sive amici, id est, justorum spiritus auscultant, quos de tuo coetu ad me in coelestia assumpsi. Ipsi enim de tua praedicatione et fratrum salute congaudent. Respondet confestim 1105Cipsa sponsa dilecto suo.

Vers. 14. Fuge, dilecte mi, et assimilare capreae hinnuloque cervorum super montes aromatum. Mira responsio. Invitatur ad praedicandum, et ut sponsus audiat vocem ejus; illa vero hortatur sponsum suum 1106Aut fugiat. Sed magna est mysterii consequentia. Nam quia superius praedicta fuerat Christi nativitas cum dixit: Quis det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera: praedicta enim fuerat ejus passio, ubi dictum est: Sub arbore malo suscitavi te; nunc consequenter hortatur sponsum ut fugiat, hoc est, velocissime per ascensionem ad Patrem redeat. Et est sensus: jubes me praedicare, vis vocem meam audire, fuge, dilecte mi, id est, impleto incarnationis tuae et passionis mysterio, revertere ad coelum; ut non jam te sicut hominem, sed sicut Deum super omnia cogitare incipiam. Tunc enim audies vocem meam, quia tunc te liberius omni mundo praedicabo. Et hoc est quod Dominus in Evangelio dicit: Ego veritatem dico vobis, expedit vobis ut ego vadam (Joan. XVI, 7). Expediebat enim apostolis et omni Ecclesiae ut Christus rediret, et praesentiam corporalem absentaret: quia dum in corpore praesens erat, non poterant eum spiritualiter amare, nec poterant eum, quem hominem cernebant, ut Deum invisibilem et ubique praesentem cogitare. 1106B535 At postquam ipse coelos ascendit, coepit eum Ecclesia spiritualiter diligere, et sic jam Deum cogitare et praedicare. Et hoc est quod Apostolus dicit: Etsi noveramus Christum secundum carnem, sed nunc jam eum non novimus (II Cor. V, 16). Ergo, dilecte mi, si vis ita me te praedicare, fuge, id est, recede ad coelestia; et qui fuisti comprehensibilis per humanitatem, efficere incomprehensibilis per divinitatem. Verumtamen ne me in praesenti sine tua visitatione deseras, sed dignare me crebra tua visitatione requirere, mihique te saepius videndum praebere. Et hoc est quod dicit: Similis esto capreae hinnuloque cervorum super montes aromatum. Sicut (inquit) capreae et hinauli cervorum, indomita animalia, humanum consortium refugiunt, et tamen crebrius in montibus videntur: sic et tu post tuam ascensionem, licet invisibilis sis, dignare mihi tamen saepius dono tuae gratiae apparere, et hoc super montes aromatum. Montes aromatum sancti Dei sunt, excellentia sanctae conversationis coelo proximi, et 1106Codore ac fama virtutum longe lateque fragrantes; hinc Apostolus de se suique similibus dicit: Nostra conversatio in coelis est (Philip. III, 20); et alibi: Christi bonus odor sumus Deo in omni loco (II Cor. II, 1).

CHAPTER VIII.

Verse 1. Who shall give thee to me for my brother, sucking the breasts of my mother? From these words it is understood that this whole song is divine and spiritual, and has in itself nothing that can be understood according to the letter. For what woman in love ever wished this, that he whom she loves should be made a little child, and her brother; and, turned into an infant, should suck the breasts of her mother? It is therefore the voice of that Church which went before the coming of Christ, which we call the Synagogue, that is, the congregation. Who, she says, shall give thee to me for my brother, sucking the breasts of 1100Amy mother? And the sense is: Thou who art now in the bosom of the Father (Joan. I, 18), who shall grant that thou be made man, and, being partaker and consort of my nature, be called my brother? For he himself deigned to call himself the brother of the apostles, saying through the Psalmist: I will declare thy name to my brethren (Psal. XXI, 23); and after the resurrection, to the women: Go, he says, tell my brethren (Matth. XXVIII, 10). Sucking the breasts of my mother, she says, that is, fulfilling all things that belong to human nature, sin excepted. For the mother of the Synagogue in this place is understood to be human nature itself, from which the Synagogue herself arose. Sucking, she says, the breasts of my mother, that is, bearing all things that pertain to human frailty.

That I may find thee without, that is, that I may see thee made man in the open. For God was as it were within, since he was already hidden in the bosom of the Father's majesty; but he was found without when, made man, he showed himself visible to men, and, being incomprehensible, willed to be comprehended.

And I may kiss thee. And I may kiss thee, she says, that is, that I may behold 1100Bthee with a manifest vision, whom now I hold only in the kiss of faith; which she wished at the beginning of this song, saying: Let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth (Cant. I, 1), that is, let him now come by himself, that he may appear to me. For the beloved was as it were kissing the Church through the prophets, when he promised his coming through them. She wishes, therefore, that coming by himself he may kiss her, that is, that he may show his presence in his own flesh.

And now let no man despise me. For the Synagogue was as it were despised before Christ came: because, given over to the observances of the law, she preached the coming of Christ under type and figure, and not in truth. But after Jesus appeared and enlightened the world by the grace of his coming, now no man despised her; nay rather, she was terrible as a battle-line of the camps set in order to her enemies: because through the whole world she preached Christ, and subjected the worldly empire to herself. Nor ought it to seem strange if we now call the Church the Synagogue; for the Church is one in the parts that go before and 1100Cin those that follow.

Verse 2. I will take hold of thee, and will lead thee into my mother's house. O beloved! by cleaving to thee through faith and charity, I will lead thee into my mother's house. The mother of the Church, in this place, is understood to be that heavenly Jerusalem of which Paul says: But that Jerusalem which is above is free, which is our mother (Gal. IV, 26). I will lead thee, she says, into my mother's house, that is, I will follow thee returning into heaven, after the act of thy incarnation and the mystery of thy passion. And I will lead thee, she says, stands for this: I will escort and follow; because the apostles escorted with their eyes the Lord ascending into the heavens, and followed him with a devout gaze even until the cloud received him, until the angels standing by said: Ye men of Galilee, why stand you looking up to heaven? This Jesus who is taken up from you into heaven shall so come as you have seen him going into heaven (Act. I, 11).

There thou shalt teach me. This refers back to what is above, where it is said, I may find thee without. When, she says, I shall have found thee without, 1100Dthat is, when I shall have beheld thee in the flesh thou hast assumed, there thou shalt teach me: because thou wilt answer to all things that I shall wish to ask. For often too the Lord anticipated the apostles when they wished to ask, and instructed them (Marc. IX). It may also be joined to this place, where he says, I will lead thee into my mother's house, there thou shalt teach me. When, she says, thou shalt have been received into heaven, and shalt have taken me also up with thee into heaven, there thou shalt teach me, and wilt lead me into all truth — which it is impossible for man to be able to attain in the present life. Hence it is that the Lord promises to the apostles, saying: The hour cometh when I will no more speak to you in proverbs, but will show you plainly of the Father (Joan. XVI, 25). Therefore there thou shalt teach me, that is, thou wilt reveal thyself and the Father to me in open vision; that is, thou wilt show me thy divinity face to face.

And I will give thee a cup of spiced wine. By the wine is understood the fervour of charity and love. 532 I will give thee wine, she says, that is, I will show with how most fervent a charity I love thee. And this wine will not be plain, 1101Abut spiced, namely with good works and virtues. For the love of God, as the blessed Gregory says (S. Gregorius, homil. 30 in Evang.), is never idle; it works great things if it is there; for if it refuses to work, it is not love. For spiced wine is love together with work. Whence also the Lord in the Gospel: If any man love me, he will keep my word (Joan. XIV, 23). The proof, therefore, of love is the showing forth of work.

And the new wine of my pomegranates. The malum granatum is the same as the malum punicum. Now it is called malum granatum because it is full of a multitude of grains (grana); malum punicum, because it abounds most plentifully in Africa. For Punica is Africa itself. And it has a rind of a blood-red colour, and therefore (as has often been said) it holds the figure of the Lord's passion. For the new wine of pomegranates is the love of the holy martyrs, who loved God so greatly that they did not hesitate to die for him. And fittingly is it called new wine, and not any other draught, because new wine 1101Bis most fervent, so that in its exceeding ferment it boils out and purges from itself every impurity. The Church, therefore, will give to the Beloved the new wine of her pomegranates, that is, she will show in the sufferings of the holy martyrs with how great a charity Christ is loved.

Verse 3. His left hand under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me. By the left hand (as the blessed Gregory says) are understood all the prosperities of the present life (S. Greg. in cap. II Cant. cant.); but by the right hand is understood the blessed life to come in the heavens. The Church, therefore, has the left hand of the bridegroom under her head, and the right hand embraces her: because whatever prosperities of the present world allure her, she counts for nothing; all things that smile upon her for a time she despises; and while she strives toward the vision of the heavenly blessedness alone, that alone she desires to embrace.

Verse 4. I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, that you stir not up, nor make the beloved to awake, till she herself will. The Church has certain persons within her who have been 1101Cborn again by the baptism of Christ and are entitled with the name of Christianity, yet by their manners and their manner of life serve the world, and strive to disquiet the Church lulled in a most sweet rest, and to rouse her to the enduring of tumults. These, therefore, on account of the grace of the mysteries of Christ which they have received, are called in this place daughters of Jerusalem, although by their manners they do the contrary. And the bridegroom adjures them not to stir up nor to make the beloved to awake, that is, that they should not by their own restlessness rouse her, set as she is in the sleep of a most sweet quiet, that is, in the contemplation of her Creator, and compel her to awake unto the anxieties and cares of the world. Till she herself will, that is, until, the office of her due service being performed, whereby she serves God in psalms, hymns, and spiritual canticles, she be admonished again by the care of bodily frailty to return to the use of the world.

Verse 5. Who is this that cometh up from the desert, flowing with delights, leaning upon her beloved? The voice of the Synagogue marvelling at the Church gathered from the nations. Who is, she says, this, that is, of what sort is she, of how great merit, that cometh up from the desert? For she had been forsaken by God 1101Dthrough idolatry and ignorance of the truth. She was ascending, therefore, by the steps of the virtues out of the desert, that is, out of the error of unbelief, in which for a time she had been forsaken. Flowing with delights, that is, abounding in virtues. Leaning, she says, upon her beloved, that is, acting with confidence in Christ, and referring all her virtues and all her goods to his grace. She knew that by her own strength she could accomplish nothing of good, and therefore she says with the Apostle: By the grace of God I am what I am (I Cor. XV, 10). And it is to be noted that she says, upon her beloved: for the Synagogue thought that she alone had the knowledge of God, the rest of the nations being left in ignorance. For God is known, as the Psalmist says, in Judea; in Israel his name is great (Psal. LXXV, 2). But to the Synagogue thus marvelling her own bridegroom and beloved answers what follows.

Under the apple tree I raised thee up. By the apple tree we ought to understand the Lord's cross, under which tree the Synagogue was raised up: because she was redeemed from the transgression of original sin, and from the power of the devil, by the cross of 1102AChrist: there was she raised up who was dead in her sins. And fittingly does he say the apple tree; for above also she compared her beloved Christ to this tree, saying: As the apple tree among the trees of the woods, so is my beloved among the sons.

There was thy mother corrupted, there was she that bore thee violated. By the mother and her that bore the Synagogue he means the greater and more principal part of the Jewish people, such as were the scribes and Pharisees, who delivered Christ to Pilate (Matth. XXVII, 2), and who as mother and bearer ought to have ruled and instructed the rest of the people; and such as were those who, at the urging of those chief men, shamelessly cried out: Crucify him, crucify him (Joan. XIX, 6). This mother, therefore, of the Synagogue was corrupted and violated under the apple tree, when she bound herself and her posterity with a cruel curse, saying: His blood be upon us and upon our children (Matth. XXVII, 25). For they too were under the cross of Christ — not indeed in faith and devotion, yet by the imprecation of a cruel vengeance upon themselves and upon their posterity.

1102BVerse 6. Set me as a seal upon thy heart, as a seal upon thy arm. Because, he says, under the apple tree I raised thee up, that is, because in my passion on the cross I redeemed thee, Set me as a seal upon thy heart, that is, have me always in memory, and forget not me, how great things I bore for thy salvation. Set me, he says, as a seal upon thy heart by faith; set me as a seal upon thy arm by worthy work. For we also, when we wish to hold something in memory, are wont to put a seal upon a finger or upon the arm, so that, admonished by it, we may remember that matter of which we have the care. And we ought to set Christ as a seal upon our heart and upon our arm, that we may always have his memory, and that, believing rightly in him, we may adorn that very faith with good works. And if we shall have done this, he too will take us up as a seal, and will remember us, according to what he promises to Zorobabel, leader of Judea: In that day I will take thee, O Zorobabel my servant, and will set thee 1102Cas a seal before my face (Aggaei II, 24). For if we shall have forgotten his benefits, and shall not have rendered him worthy thanksgivings, he too 533 will forget us, as is said of the impious king Jechonias: If Jechonias the son of Joachim king of Juda were a ring on my right hand, I would pluck him thence (Jer. XXII, 24).

For love is strong as death, jealousy as hard as hell. The love of Christ is strong as death. For as death separates the soul from the body, and it is no longer permitted to covet anything, nor to strive after anything in the present life: so the love of Christ, whomsoever it has truly pervaded, wholly mortifies him to this world and renders him as it were without feeling; and living for Christ alone, he is dead to the world. The sense is the same when he says, jealousy as hard as hell. For as hell never restores those whom it has once received, but ever retains them: so the true love of Christ never lets go him whom it has once received; whence the Apostle says: Who shall separate us from the charity of Christ, tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or 1102Dnakedness, or danger, or the sword (Rom. VIII, 35)? But the love of Christ may also be understood in this place, whose love was strong as death: because he so loved us that, being immortal, he took death upon himself for us. His jealousy was hard as hell, because hell knows not how to have mercy, nor to be softened by the punishments of the wretched: so also the love of Christ could not cease from mercy, nor be removed by any wickednesses of persecutors from the love of the human race. Some refer this which is said, jealousy is hard as hell, properly to the Synagogue, which was jealous of the nations and envied their salvation.
